Thank you for your recent undated letter to RAF West Drayton on the subject of
Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs), in which you requested information for a
school project. The letter has been passed to the Ministry of Defence for reply.

You may find it helpful if I begin by explaining that while the Ministry of
Defence does receive and co-ordinate some reports of UFO sightings, our sole
concern is to establish whether or not they pose a threat to the security of the
United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and this has not been the case so
far, we do not attempt to investigate further, or to identify whatever might have

been seen.

Given this limited involvement with the subject, the Ministry of Defence has no
department which is appointed solely for the purpose of studying UFO reports, nor
are there any staff employed on the subject full time. The reports we receive,
which are usually limited to very brief details of what was witnessed, are passed
to staff in the departments concerned with the air defence of the United Kingdom,
who examine them.as a part of their normal duties.

It is clear from the reports we receive that there are many strange things to be
seen in the sky. However, we believe that rational explanations could be found
for most of them. Possibilities that spring to mind include aircraft seen from
unusual angles, weather balloons, satellites, or unusual cloud formations to name
but a few. Certainly, we have yet to receive conclusive evidence that alien
spacecraft have ever landed on the planet.

If you are interested in obtaining further information on this subject, you might
like to contact some of the many civilian organisations currently engaged in
study.of the UFO phenomenon. May I suggest the following societies:

Thank you for your letter of 13 November on the subject of Unidentified Flying
Objects,in which you requested any information we might be able to provide for

a book you are producing.

Our reference

You may find it helpful if I begin by explaining that while the Ministry of
Defence does receive and co-ordinate some reports of UFO sightings, our sole
concern is to determine whether or not they present a threat to the security
of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and this has not been the
case so far, we do not attempt to investigate further, or to identify whatever

might have been seen.

Given this limited involvement with the subject, the Ministry of Defence has

no department which is appointed solely for the purpose of studying UFO reports,
nor are there any staff employed on the subject full time. The reports we
receive, which are usually limited to very brief details of what was witnessed,
are passed to staff in the departments concerned with the air defence of the
United Kingdom, who examine them as a part of their normal duties. The Ministry
of Defence does not carry out studies, investigations or surveys into the UFO

phenomenon.

It is clear from the reports we receive that there are many strange things to

be seen in the sky. However, we believe that rational explanations could be

found for most of them. Possibilities that spring to mind include meteorological
balloons, satellites, aircraft seen from unusual angles or unusual cloud formations,
to name but a few. Certainly, we have yet to receive conclusive evidence that
alien spacecraft have ever landed on the planet.

With regard to your request for information about sightings in Kent, I regret
that our reports are not filed in such a way as to make it easy to identify
those originating in a particular area of the country. However, I can tell you
that in the past five years, the following numbers of reports have been received?

1989 258
1988 397
1987 150
1986 120
1985 177

So far, this year, the total stands at.190 reports.

Thank you for your letter of ist October to the Secretary of
State for Defence on the subject.of Unidentified Flying Objects
(UFOs). I have been asked to reply.

You may find it helpful if I begin by explaining that while the
Ministry of Defence does receive and co-ordinate some reports
of UFO sightings, our sole concern is to determine whether they
present a threat to the security of the UK. Unless we judge
that they do, and this has not been the case so far, we make no
attempt to investigate further, or to identify whatever might
have been seen.

Given this limited involvement with the subject, the Ministry
of Defence has no department which is appointed solely for the
purpose of studying UFO reports, nor are there any staff
employed on the subject full time. The reports we receive,
which are usually. limited to very brief details of what was
witnessed, are passed to staff in the departments concerned
with the air defence of the UK, who examine them as a part of
their normal duties. The Ministry of Defence does not carry
out studies, investigations or surveys into the UFO phenomenon.

It is clear from the reports we receive that there are many
strange things to be seen in the sky. However, we believe that
rational explanations could be found for most of them.
Possibilities that spring to mind include meteorological
balloons, satellite debris re-entering the atmosphere, or air-
craft seen from unusual angles, to name but a few. Indeed we
have yet to receive conclusive evidence that alien spacecraft
have landed on the planet.

Defence has not received any reports of unusual sightings
around Bodmin.

Peeegoection 40 |

I am currently investigating an aerial phenomenon that occured. over the
Cornish coast on Sunday evening 2nd September '90. The event caused some
alarm to a number of people and was reported to the Camborne police. In
brief the sighting concerned a number of orange lights that appeared over
verious points along the coast and over the village of Brill. There was
also two reports of some brilliant white lights seen over Mounts Bay and
off the coast at Falmouth. The white lights were without doubt associated
with a helicopter that was observed at about 2,000' dropping white flares.
The orange lights were observed in all cases at approx 200' but no aircraft
appears to have been seen.

From my correspondence with RAF St Mawgan and RNAS Culdrose I have estab-
lished that they did not have any aircraft airbourne at the time of the
sightings - 20.30hrs to 22.30hrs. Culdrose did inform me that ships of the
Royal Navy were on exercise over the weekend Ist/2nd September '90 and that
the aircraft carrier H.M.S. Invincible was one of them. Invincible does of
course carry helicopters and it's quite possible that her helicopters could
have been engaged in an exercise using illumination flares, in particular
white ones. I have been unable to establish if any orange coloured flares
were in use but of course the use of orange flares , by the military is

not unknown. :

In my initial report to the British UFO Research Association, of which I

am an accredited investigator, I proposed two possible scenarios for the
orange lights, they are as follows. Firstly, they were orange marker flares
dropped after the deployment of white flares by military aircraft (helicop-
ters) whilst carring out exercises. This would make sense of the sightings
and their eventually disappearance in a sequence. The second scenario
presents a possible UFO event along the coast starting at Lands End (first
sighting) at 20.30hrs and ending at Gorran Haven at 22.15hrs (last sight-
ing). If the orange lights were a UFO or UFOs its possible that the mili-
tary having been informed of their presence were deployed to search for
them using illumination flares. This possibility is enforced by the fact
that on Monday evening the 3rd of September 190 a flight of 6 helicopters
were observed over the village of Brill carring out what appeared to be a
search type operation!. Brill is the only land sight where the orange
lights were seen.

On the advice of Section 40 AER I please ask through your good offices

ff spec cele eet

Dear Sir(s),

I am writing to you with the hope that you will be able to

help me. JI am an official investigator for Quest International.
The organisation is dedicated to the scientific research

of Unidentified Flying Object phenomenon.

Quest International recognise the fact that no single aspect

of the phenomenon should be overlooked or adhered to. The
organisation therefore has no single viewpoint or hypothesis
regarding UFO's, but studies many different options and research
categories.

By liaising and co-operating with scientific and military
sources we have determined that as much as 20% of all reports
cannot be readily identified.

The reason I am writing to you is to ask for your co-operation
with Bristol Quest's current investigations. Since the
beginning of February 1990, we have been receiving numerous
reports from the public of Unidentified Flying Object's that
have been witnessed above the city of Bristol.

Each report we received was automatically checked with Bristol
Airport's Air Traffic Control and the regional police station
involved in the area of the sighting. What I am asking is

if it would be possible for you to forward to me any information
you may have on any UFO sightings your department received

with regards to Bristol.

I must stress that all information we receive is held in

the strictest of confidence. Any information you are able
to forward will be gratefully received.

Yours Sincerely,

ARMY INVOLVEMENT IN CORN CIRCLE INVESTIGATIONS

1. Thank you for your memo of the 5 October, and for protecti
m_ the dubious pleasure of corresponding directly with SERN 40

I am unaware of the assistance which the Army
allegedly gave during "Project Blackbird", and frankly I am
reluctant to start hares running in an attempt_to find out. The
following paragraphs therefore concentrate on feeble
assertions that Army helicopters have been caught photographing
corn circles. I hope you can make use of them.

Helicopters from the Army Air Corps flying school in Middle
Wallop are a common sight over many. of the farms in the
Wiltshire/Hampshire/Berkshire area. Much of the training which
pilots from the school undertake involves practising standard
rotary wing manoeuvres such as moving to and from a prolonged
hover. Where possible manoeuvres of that kind would normally be
carried out well away from houses and livestock, and so a
patient observer would stand a good chance of sighting a
helicopter hovering above a field where corn circles have
appeared. I respectfully suggest that this may be all that the
photographs attached to your letter show.

You are, of course, quite entitled to your own theories, but I
am afraid I can offer you nothing that gives substance to them.
I can only repeat that neither the British Army nor the
Ministry of Defence have the slightest interest in solving this
conundrum, intriguing though it may be.

2. I would be grateful for a copy of your response.

GS Sec 1d

Dear Sir,
tT write as the North London investigator for the British Unidentified

Flying Object Research Association (BUFORA): to seek your assistance concerning

a sighting of a spindle shaped spinning object with associated objects at North

Harrow on I4th July this year.

My informant and his two sons viewed the object at 17.15 hours in clear weather
conditions; they estimated the altitude of the object to be between 3,000 and
3000 feet. The object appeared to be reflecting the sun, and after a few seconds
apparently spawned several smaller objects. It moved slowly in an ESE to NNW
direction; there/a number of aircraft in the sky at the time but the object did
not resemble an aircraft. :

Since the object reflected the sun and appeared solid, I would be interested
to know whether your radar registered its presence or that of the associated
objects. If so, are you able to. say what the object might have been?

Please accept my thanks in anticipation of your assistance.

Yrs sincerely,

The Controller

Dear Sir(s),

I am writing to you in the hope that you will be able to help

me. I am the Area Regional Co-Ordinator for Quest International.
The organisation is dedicated to the scientific research.of

| Unidentified Flying Object phenomenon.

Quest International recognise. the fact that no Single aspect

of the phenomenon should be overlooked or adhered to. The
organisation therefore has no single viewpoint or hypothesis.
regarding UFO's, but studies many different options and research
categories. :

By liaising and co-operating with scientific and military sources
we have determined that as much as 20% of all reports cannot
be readily identified.

The reason I am writing to you is to ask for your .co-operation
with my teams current investigations. Since the beginning of
February 1990, we have been receiving numerous reports from
the public of Unidentified Flying Object's that have been
witnessed above the city of Bristol.

Each report we received was automatically checked with Bristol
Airport's Air Traffic Control and the regional police station
involved im the area of the sighting. What I am asking is if
it would be possible for you-to forward me any information you
may have concerning UFO reports over Bristol or the surrounding
area's,

I must stress that all information we receive is held in the
strictest of confidence.

Any information you are able to forward will be gratefully received.

Yours Sincerely,

Daw Pection 40 |

Thank you for your letter of 18 September on the. subject of

Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs), in which you requested

any information we might be able to provide on unusual sightings
in the Bristol area.

As you are probably aware from previous correspondence, the
Ministry of Defence has only a very limited involvement with
this subject. As you state, Ministry of Defence files are
subject to the Public Records Act, which prohibits. the release
of reports en bloc. Therefore, it is not usually possible

to help with requests as general as yours. However, if you
are able to provide us with dates and times of sightings in which
you are interested, we are able to check whether we have any
similar reports on file, and if so, can let you know what they
contain. I should, however, point out that due to a lack of
staff and resources, we can only do this for reports received
during the last three years.

Given these conditions, if you feel we may be able to be of
help, please do not hesitate to contact us again.

-fhank you for your report to RAF Finningley of an unusual sighting you witnessed
on 28 August, which has been passed to the Ministry of Defence for retention
and reply.

You may find it helpful if I begin by explaining that while the Ministry of Defence
does receive and co-ordinate some reports of UFO sightings, our sole concern is to
determine whether or not they present a threat to the security of the United
Kingdom. . Unless we judge that they do, and this has not been the case so far, we
do not attempt to investigate further, or to identify whatever might have been
seen.

Given this limited involvement with the subject, the Ministry of Defence has no~
department which is appointed solely for the purpose of studying UFO reports, nor
are there any ‘staff empl @a on the subject full time. The reports we ‘receiv
which are usually limited.to very brief details of what was witnessed, are passed
to staff in the departments concerned with the air defence of the UK, who examine -
them as a part of their ‘hormal duties. The Ministry of Defence does not carry out
studies, investigations or surveys into the UFO phenomenen, nor do we have the |
resources to do So. am sure you will appreciate that Defence Funds are already
fully committed t¢ maintaining the security of the UK, and_ meetin resp

on a scientific investigation unless a t
been established. . Es

ponsibilites a

€ the UK had

In this context, T regret that I am unable to offer a definitive ‘explanation for
what you witnessed. Nevertheless, we are grateful to you for having taken the
time to make a report. .

I hope that you will find this helpful.

September 18th 1990.

Dear Sir(s),

Jo am writing to you with a request that I hope you will be able
to help me with. I am the Area Regional Co-Ordinator for

Quest International, an organisation dedicated to the scientific
research of Unidentified Flying Object phenomenon and all such
related aspects. :

Bristol Quest is the local offshoot of Quest International and
comprises of a team of official investigators who work on behalf
of the named organisation.

Bristol Quest is currently involved in gathering all information
and.reports of UFO's witnessed above the city and surrounding
areas. I realise that the Ministry Of Defence policy on UFO
reports is subject to the Public Records Act, but I would be
very grateful if you have any such reports regarding sightings
around the Bristol area that you would be able to forward to

me inorder for us to include them in our research.

Yours Sincerely,

Area Regional Co-Ordinator.

Thank you for your recént letter on the subject of Unidentified
Flying Objects (UFOs), in which you requested any information
we might be able to provide.

You may find it helpful if I begin by explaining that while the
Ministry of Defence does receive and co-ordinate some reports
of UFO sightings our sole concern is to determine whether or
not they present a threat to the security of the United Kingdom.
Unless we judge that they do, and. this has not_been the case _
so far, we do not attempt to investigate further or to identify
whatever might have been seen.

Given this limited involvement with the subject, the Ministry of
Defence has no department which is appointed solely for the
purpose of studying UFO reports, nor are there any staff employed
on the subject full-time. The reports we receive, which are
usually limited to very brief details of what was witnessed,

Thank you for your letter of 25 July to the Prime Minister on the subject of
Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs) which has been passed to the Ministry of
Defence for reply. :

You may find it helpful if I begin by explaining that while the Ministry of
Defence does receive and co-ordinate some reports of UFO sightings, our sole
concern is to determine whether or not they present a threat to the defences of
the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and this has not been the case
so far, we do not attempt to investigate further, or to identify whatever might
have been. seen. /

Given this limited involvement with the subject,.the Ministry of Defence has no
department which is appointed solely for the purpose of studying UFO reports,
nor are there any staff employed on the subject full time. The reports we
receive, which are usually limited to very brief details of what was witnessed,
are passed to staff in the departments concerned with the air defence of the UK,
who examine them as a part of their normal duties.

It is clear from the reports we réceive that there are many strange things to be
seen in the skies. However, we believe that rational explanations could be found
for most of them. Possibilities that spring to mind include aircraft seen from
unusual angles, satellite debris re-entering the atmosphere or weather balloons
to name but a few.

All the UFO reports the Ministry of Defence receives are unclassified. However,
in common with other Government Departments,*Ministry of Defence files are

subject to the Public Records Act. This Act states that all files, be they
classified or unclassified, are to remain closed to general viewing until 30 years
after the last action has been taken on them. We have been able to establish

from previous enquiries that there are some files over 30 years old containing
reports of unusual sightings in the Public Records Office at Kew; you mignt care
to contact them direct for further information.

.I hope this is helpful.

Thank you for your letter of 27 July on the subject of
Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs).

It may be helpful if I begin by explaining that while the
Ministry of Defence does receive and co-ordinate some information
about UFO sightings, our sole concern is to determine whether or
not they present a threat to the security and defence of the
United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and this has-not
been the case so far, no attempt is: made by MoD to investigate or
identify the objects.

Given this limited involvement with the subject, the Ministry of
Defence has no department which is appointed solely for the
purpose of studying UFO reports, nor are there any staff employed
on the subject full time. The reports we receive, which are
usually limited to very brief details of what was witnessed, are
examined by staff in the departments concerned with the UK’s air
defence as a part of their normal duties. .

The Ministry of Defence does not carry out studies,
investigations or surveys into the UFO phenomenon, nor do we have
the resources to do so. I am sure that you will appreciate that
Defence funds are already committed to maintaining the security
of the UK and meeting our responsibilities to allies and
dependent territories. Therefore, we could not justify their use
on scientific investigations unless a clear threat to security
had been established. It therefore follows that the majority of
sightings passed to us remain, by definition, unidentified,
though we believe that rational explanations could be found for
most of them. Possibilities that spring to mind include
satellite debris, meteorological balloons and aircraft seen from
unusual angles, to name but a few. Additionally, you will be
interested to know that aircraft, both civil and military, carry
both red and white flashing navigation lights.

t hope this is helpful. a
Yous Ainesrebin,

17 July 1990

On 10 July you wrote to Gatwick reporting the siting of "a
flashing light" travelling at speed in various directions in the
Worthing area. You mentioned that you had previously seen this
phenomenon and that other people had also witnessed the sitings.

Your letter has been passed to me at the Headquarters National
Air Traffic Services for action. There is a department at the
London Air Traffic Control Centre with specific responsibilities
for investigating such sitings. Might I therefore suggest that,
in the first instance, you contact the department by telephone

LORD TREFGARNE — PARTICIPATION IN UFO DOCUMENTARY

Reference: D/US of S(AF) ADG 9/1/2/5 dated 18 July 1990

1. I attach a draft letter for you to send to Lord Trefgarne’s
Diary Secretary regarding his request for advice from MoD
following an invitation to appear in a documentary about the
Aetherius Society and the UFO phenomena.

2. The MoD’s line on UFOs, which has remained consistent over
many years, stresses the strictly limited nature of the
Department’s interest in the topic.

3. The Aetherius Society is a nonprofit organisation which was
founded in the UK in 1955-6 by a who claims to be in
contact with extraterrestrial intelligences. The group’s beliefs
include the idea that UFOs are craft from Venus, Mars and other
planets in Earth’s solar system. The aim of the group is to
promote the teachings of these cosmic intelligences and to
prepare Earth's inhabitants for the coming of the next great
master.

4. Given the limited nature of our involvement with the subject,
and the tenets of the Aetherius Society, unless Lord Trefgarne
has a personal interest in the subject and wishes to speak on his
own behalf, we would not recommend that he appear in the
documentary as an official representative of the Government.

5. I hope that this is helpful. If you need any further advice
please do not hesitate to contact me.

. Sec(AS)2a

DRAFT LETTER TO LORD TREFGARNE'S DIARY SECRETARY ON UFOS

Thank you for your letter of 18 July regarding the invitation
which was sent to Lord Trefgarne asking him to appear in a
documentary on the subjects of the Aetherius Society and

Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs).

You may find it helpful if I begin by stating the MoD position
with regard to the subject of UFOs. Although some information
about UFOs is received and coordinated within the Department, our
main concern is to determine whether or not UFOs present a threat
to the security and defence of the United Kingdom. Reports are
examined within the MoD solely to see whether they have any
defence implications, and once it is clear they do not, we do not
pursue our research further. The MoD has no department which is
appointed solely for the purpose of studying UFO reports and we
have no staff employed on the subject full time. We do not carry
out full time studies or scientific investigations into the UFO

phenomena.

The Aetherius Society is a nonprofit organisation which was
founded in the United Kingdom in the mid-1950s by Fysection40 |
Section 40 FIER claimed to be in contact with various ;
extraterrestrial intelligences. The group%s beliefs include the
notion that UFOs are craft from Venus, Mars and other planets in

Earth’s solar system. The aim of this group is to promote the
teachings of these cosmic intelligences and to prepare Earth’s

inhabitants for the coming of the next great master.

Given the limited nature of the MoD’s involvement with the
subject of UFOs, and the beliefs of the Aetherius Society, unless
Lord Trefgarne has a particular personal interest in the subject
and wishes to speak on his. own behalf; the MoD would not
recommend that he apppear in the documentary as an official

representative of the Government.

I hope that this proves useful.

Thank you for your letter of 25 June to Sir Michael Quinlan on the
subject of Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs), in which you
requested any information we might be able to provide.

It may be helpful if I begin by explaining that while the
Ministry of Defence does receive and co-ordinate some reports of
UFO sightings, our sole concern is to determine whether or not
they present a threat to the security. of the United Kingdom.
Unless we judge that they do, and this has not been the case so
far, we do not attempt to investigate further, or to identify
whatever might have been seen.

Given this limited involvement with the subject, the Ministry of
Defence has no department which is appointed solely for the
purpose of studying UFO reports, nor are there any staff employed
on the subject full time. The reports we receive, which are
usually limited to very brief details of what was witnessed, are
passed to staff in the departments concerned with the air defence
of the United Kingdom, who examine them as a part of their normal
duties. The Ministry of Defence does not carry out studies,
investigations or surveys into the phenomenon, nor do we have the
resources to do so. I am sure you will accept that Defence funds
are already fully committed to maintaining the security of the UK
and meeting our responsibilities to allies and dependent
territories. This being so, we could not justify their use on
scientific investigations unless a clear threat to the security
of the UK had béen identified.
'%®
Turning to your detailed questions about reports, it is correct
to say that all the -reports the Ministry of Defence receives are
unclassified. However, in common with other Government Departments,
all Ministry of Defence files, be they classified or unclassified,
are subject. to the Public Records Act. This Act states that all
files are to remain closed to general viewing until 30 years after
the last action has been taken on them. It is also correct
that prior to 1967, the Ministry of Defence maintained a policy
of destroying all UFO reports after 5 years. However, since
1967, reports have been preserved. We have been able to establish
from previous enquiries that some files containing reports of
unusual sightapare available for inspection in the Public Records
Office at Kew? you may find it useful to contact them direct for
more information.

I hope that you will find this helpful.

Having investigated this UFO report without succeeding in
attributing its stimulus to any mundane causes I am, therefore,
writing to you in order to ascertain whether your department
received any contemporaneous reports from the area in question
which might shed some light on the matter.

Of course, I do realise that your jurisdiction vis~a-vis
the UFO controversy is strictly confined within the parameters
of defence and I am not soliciting for your comments but even a
simple listing of incoming reports helps to put the situation
into perspective.

Any information you could possibly give me in regard to
this case, even if only in the negative, would be of immense
help in the course of my enquiries.

Looking forward to your anticipated response.
